Use the absolute paths above.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Project Map\\r\\n\\r\\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\\r\\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\\r\\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\\r\\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\\r\\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\\r\\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\\r\\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\\r\\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Stack And Commands\\r\\n\\r\\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\\r\\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\\r\\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\\r\\n- Build: `npm run build`\\r\\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\\r\\n- Test: `npm run test`\\r\\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\\r\\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\\r\\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Working Rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\\r\\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\\r\\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\\r\\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\\r\\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\\r\\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\\r\\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\\r\\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Product Constraints\\r\\n\\r\\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\\r\\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\\r\\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\\r\\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\\r\\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Smart Drill Note\\r\\n\\r\\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\r\\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed \\"fix\\" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workflow Expectations\\r\\n\\r\\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\\r\\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\\r\\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\\r\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\\r\\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s \\"Revert to commit\\", \\"revert and resend\\", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental \\"revert and resend\\" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\\r\\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\\r\\n\\r\\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\\r\\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong \\"Done\\" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\\r\\n\\r\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Codex Adapter Layout\\r\\n\\r\\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\\r\\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.\\r\\n- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Known Gaps\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\\r\\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.\\r\\n- `README.md` is still minimal.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\r\\n"}}'}]

A caller that must not write over a finished run —\\r\\n * a homework assignment, whose row is that one sitting — stops calling before\\r\\n * it gets here (see genericFinalizedRef in Drill.tsx).\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function savePracticeSessionSnapshot(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  pool: Pick<PracticePool, \'slug\' | \'completionId\'>,\\r\\n  snapshot: PracticeSessionSnapshot,\\r\\n): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  if (!userId || !pool?.slug) return;\\r\\n  if (snapshot.questionQueue.length === 0) return;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const id = practiceSessionIdFor(userId, pool.slug);\\r\\n  await withRow(id, async () => {\\r\\n    const persist = getPersistence(userId);\\r\\n    const existing = await loadPracticeSession(userId, pool.slug);\\r\\n\\r\\n    const record = PracticeSession.parse({\\r\\n      id,\\r\\n      user_id: userId,\\r\\n      pool_slug: pool.slug,\\r\\n      completion_id: pool.completionId,\\r\\n      data_version: PRACTICE_SESSION_DATA_VERSION,\\r\\n      question_queue: snapshot.questionQueue,\\r\\n      current_index: Math.min(\\r\\n        Math.max(snapshot.currentIndex, 0),\\r\\n        Math.max(snapshot.questionQueue.length - 1, 0),\\r\\n      ),\\r\\n      attempts: snapshot.attempts,\\r\\n      status: \'in_progress\',\\r\\n      // Never hand back time already spent: a snapshot with no clock keeps\\r\\n      // whatever the row already knew rather than clearing it.\\r\\n      elapsed_ms: snapshot.elapsedMs ?? existing?.elapsed_ms,\\r\\n      timer_mode: snapshot.timerMode ?? existing?.timer_mode,\\r\\n      timer_custom_minutes: snapshot.timerCustomMinutes ?? existing?.timer_custom_minutes,\\r\\n      regulation_answered_qids:\\r\\n        snapshot.regulationAnsweredQids ?? existing?.regulation_answered_qids,\\r\\n      started_at: existing?.started_at ?? now(),\\r\\n      updated_at: now(),\\r\\n    });\\r\\n\\r\\n    await persist.set(TABLES.practice_sessions, id, record);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Mark the set finished. The row is kept rather than deleted so the index can\\r\\n * show what was answered and how it went after completion.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function completePracticeSession(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  pool: Pick<PracticePool, \'slug\' | \'completionId\'>,\\r\\n  snapshot: PracticeSessionSnapshot,\\r\\n): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  if (!userId || !pool?.slug) return;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const id = practiceSessionIdFor(userId, pool.slug);\\r\\n  await withRow(id, async () => {\\r\\n    const persist = getPersistence(userId);\\r\\n    const existing = await loadPracticeSession(userId, pool.slug);\\r\\n\\r\\n    const record = PracticeSession.parse({\\r\\n      id,\\r\\n      user_id: userId,\\r\\n      pool_slug: pool.slug,\\r\\n      completion_id: pool.completionId,\\r\\n      data_version: PRACTICE_SESSION_DATA_VERSION,\\r\\n      question_queue: snapshot.questionQueue.length\\r\\n        ? snapshot.questionQueue\\r\\n        : existing?.question_queue ?? [],\\r\\n      current_index: Math.max(snapshot.questionQueue.length - 1, 0),\\r\\n      attempts: snapshot.attempts.length ? snapshot.attempts : existing?.attempts ?? [],\\r\\n      status: \'completed\',\\r\\n      elapsed_ms: snapshot.elapsedMs ?? existing?.elapsed_ms,\\r\\n      timer_mode: snapshot.timerMode ?? existing?.timer_mode,\\r\\n      timer_custom_minutes: snapshot.timerCustomMinutes ?? existing?.timer_custom_minutes,\\r\\n      regulation_answered_qids:\\r\\n        snapshot.regulationAnsweredQids ?? existing?.regulation_answered_qids,\\r\\n      started_at: existing?.started_at ?? now(),\\r\\n      updated_at: now(),\\r\\n    });\\r\\n\\r\\n    await persist.set(TABLES.practice_sessions, id, record);\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Clear the row so a re-run of a finished set starts from question 1. */\\r\\nexport async function resetPracticeSession(userId: string, poolSlug: string): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  if (!userId || !poolSlug) return;\\r\\n  const persist = getPersistence(userId);\\r\\n  await persist.remove(TABLES.practice_sessions, practiceSessionIdFor(userId, (os error 123)\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"8ab4d0","wall_time_seconds":10.0021748,"session_id":90476,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"49bc44","wall_time_seconds":10.0047022,"session_id":91704,"original_token_count":1580,"output":"/**\\r\\n * Resume state for guided drills.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * A student who starts an assigned guided drill and leaves — taps back, closes\\r\\n * the tab, comes back after dinner — must land on the question they stopped on,\\r\\n * with the SAME questions and every answer they already gave still marked.\\r\\n * Neither React state nor URL params can do that: both die with the page, and\\r\\n * re-entering from the Classroom re-runs the level draw, which reshuffles the\\r\\n * pool and hands the student a different set of questions. That is the bug this\\r\\n * module exists to close.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * It is a thin adapter, not a second mechanism. The Intro to LR practice sets\\r\\n * already solved exactly this problem (see practiceSessionStore), and that store\\r\\n * gives us local-first writes, a debounced `bootcamp_kv` cloud upsert, zod\\r\\n * validation, and cross-device hydration. Guided drills ride the same rows,\\r\\n * namespaced by key so the two can never collide:\\r\\n *\\r\\n *     practice set   →  pool_slug \\"mc-practice-1\\"\\r\\n *     assigned drill →  pool_slug \\"guided::<assignmentId>\\"\\r\\n *     direct drill   →  pool_slug \\"guided::drill:<drillId>\\"\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The `pool_slug`/`completion_id` field names come from that store\'s schema and\\r\\n * are reused as-is rather than forked; read them as \\"which drill\\" and \\"what it\\r\\n * completes\\", not as bootcamp-only concepts.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Failure policy, inherited: reads degrade to null (a missing resume row must\\r\\n * never block starting a drill), writes surface to the caller.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nimport {\\r\\n  loadPracticeSession,\\r\\n  savePracticeSessionSnapshot,\\r\\n  completePracticeSession,\\r\\n  resetPracticeSession,\\r\\n  type PracticeSessionSnapshot,\\r\\n} from \'@/bootcamps/main-conclusion/lib/practiceSessionStore\';\\r\\nimport type { PracticeSession, PracticeSessionAttempt } from \'@/bootcamps/main-conclusion/persistence/records\';\\r\\nimport type { DrillSession } from \'@/types/drill\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const GUIDED_SESSION_PREFIX = \'guided::\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Stable identity for one question in one sitting, including closeout retries. */\\r\\nexport async function guidedAttemptId(runId: string, qid: string): Promise<string> {\\r\\n  const bytes = new Uint8Array(await crypto.subtle.digest(\'SHA-256\', new TextEncoder().encode(`${runId}:${qid}`)));\\r\\n  bytes[6] = (bytes[6] & 0x0f) | 0x40;\\r\\n  bytes[8] = (bytes[8] & 0x3f) | 0x80;\\r\\n  const hex = [...bytes.slice(0, 16)].map((byte) => byte.toString(16).padStart(2, \'0\')).join(\'\');\\r\\n  return `${hex.slice(0, 8)}-${hex.slice(8, 12)}-${hex.slice(12, 16)}-${hex.slice(16, 20)}-${hex.slice(20, 32)}`;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type GuidedDrillSession = PracticeSession;\\r\\nexport type GuidedDrillSnapshot = PracticeSessionSnapshot;\\r\\n\\r\\n/** A migrated singleton can remain in progress if its cleanup write failed. */\\r\\nexport function isLegacyGuidedSessionSuperseded(\\r\\n  legacy: GuidedDrillSession | null,\\r\\n  completed: GuidedDrillSession[],\\r\\n): boolean {\\r\\n  if (!legacy || legacy.status !== \'in_progress\') return false;\\r\\n  return completed.some((run) =>\\r\\n    run.status === \'completed\' &&\\r\\n    run.question_queue.join(\'|\') === legacy.question_queue.join(\'|\') &&\\r\\n    legacy.attempts.every((old) => run.attempts.some((attempt) =>\\r\\n      attempt.qid === old.qid && attempt.selected_answer === old.selected_answer,\\r\\n    )),\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Key one guided drill\'s resume row. The assignment id is preferred: it is what\\r\\n * makes the row per-student-per-assignment, so re-assigning the same drill later\\r\\n * gets its own clean run. Direct launches use the drill id. The slug remains a\\r\\n * legacy fallback for runs started from `/guided-drills/:slug` before the drill\\r\\n * id travelled with the launch.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function guidedSessionKey(input: {\\r\\n  assignmentId?: string | null;\\r\\n  drillId?: string | null;\\r\\n  slug?: string | null;\\r\\n}): string | null {\\r\\n  const id = input.assignmentId?.trim();\\r\\n  if (id) return `${GUIDED_SESSION_PREFIX}${id}`;\\r\\n  const drillId = input.drillId?.trim();\\r\\n  if (drillId) return `${GUIDED_SESSION_PREFIX}drill:${drillId}`;\\r\\n  const slug = input.slug?.trim();\\r\\n  if (slug) return `${GUIDED_SESSION_PREFIX}slug:${slug}`;\\r\\n  return null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The same key, derived from what the drill runner actually has: the\\r\\n * `guidedAssignmentId` param when present, otherwise the drill identity encoded\\r\\n * in the `returnTo` path it was launched with (`/guided/:assignmentId` or\\r\\n * `/guided-drills/:slug`). Returns null for any non-guided drill, which is what\\r\\n * switches all of this off for library, flagged, and bootcamp runs.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function guidedSessionKeyForRunner(input: {\\r\\n  guidedAssignmentId?: string | null;\\r\\n  guidedDrillId?: string | null;\\r\\n  returnTo?: string | null;\\r\\n}): string | null {\\r\\n  if (input.guidedAssignmentId?.trim()) {\\r\\n    return guidedSessionKey({ assignmentId: input.guidedAssignmentId });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  if (input.guidedDrillId?.trim()) {\\r\\n    return guidedSessionKey({ drillId: input.guidedDrillId });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const path = (input.returnTo ?? \'\').split(\'?\')[0];\\r\\n  const assignmentMatch = path.match(/^\\\\/guided\\\\/([^/]+)/);\\r\\n  if (assignmentMatch) {\\r\\n    return guidedSessionKey({ assignmentId: decodeURIComponent(assignmentMatch[1]) });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const slugMatch = path.match(/^\\\\/guided-drills\\\\/([^/]+)/);\\r\\n  if (slugMatch) {\\r\\n    return guidedSessionKey({ slug: decodeURIComponent(slugMatch[1]) });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** The stored run for one guided drill, or null when there is nothing to resume. */\\r\\nexport async function loadGuidedDrillSession(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  key: string | null,\\r\\n  legacyKey?: string | null,\\r\\n): Promise<GuidedDrillSession | null> {\\r\\n  if (!userId || !key) return null;\\r\\n  const current = await loadPracticeSession(userId, key);\\r\\n  if (current || !legacyKey || legacyKey === key) return current;\\r\\n  return loadPracticeSession(userId, legacyKey);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Write where the student is right now. Called on every answer and every\\r\\n * question move, so it must stay cheap — the persistence layer writes locally at\\r\\n * once and debounces the cloud upsert.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function saveGuidedDrillSnapshot(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  key: string | null,\\r\\n  snapshot: GuidedDrillSnapshot,\\r\\n): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  if (!userId || !key) return Promise.resolve();\\r\\n  return savePracticeSessionSnapshot(userId, { slug: key, completionId: key }, snapshot);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Mark the run finished. This is what keeps a re-attempt honest: a completed row\\r\\n"}'}]

A pool that changed under a saved run does not — starting\\r\\n * clean is the only honest option there.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function isResumableGuidedSession(\\r\\n  stored: GuidedDrillSession | null,\\r\\n  queue: string[],\\r\\n): boolean {\\r\\n  if (!stored || stored.status !== \'in_progress\') return false;\\r\\n  if (stored.question_queue.length === 0) return false;\\r\\n  if (stored.question_queue.length !== queue.length) return false;\\r\\n  const target = new Set(queue);\\r\\n  return stored.question_queue.every((qid) => target.has(qid));\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Where to drop the student back in.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The stored index is a starting point, not the answer. Snapshot writes are\\r\\n * local-first with a debounced cloud upsert, so a resume that hydrates from the\\r\\n * cloud can read an index one behind the last local write and put the student\\r\\n * back on a question they already answered. Landing on the first question they\\r\\n * have NOT answered, at or after the stored index, is both immune to that race\\r\\n * and what a student expects \\"pick up where I left off\\" to mean.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Everything already answered → the last question, so they can review and\\r\\n * finish rather than being bounced past the end.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function guidedResumeIndex(\\r\\n  queue: string[],\\r\\n  storedIndex: number,\\r\\n  answeredQids: Iterable<string>,\\r\\n): number {\\r\\n  if (queue.length === 0) return 0;\\r\\n  const answered = new Set(answeredQids);\\r\\n  const start = Math.min(Math.max(storedIndex, 0), queue.length - 1);\\r\\n  if (!answered.has(queue[start])) return start;\\r\\n  for (let i = start + 1; i < queue.length; i++) {\\r\\n    if (!answered.has(queue[i])) return i;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  // Nothing unanswered ahead — fall back to anything missed earlier.\\r\\n  for (let i = 0; i < start; i++) {\\r\\n    if (!answered.has(queue[i])) return i;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return queue.length - 1;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The line shown on the launch card so the student knows their work is still\\r\\n * there. It names the question guidedResumeIndex will actually open, so the card\\r\\n * and the drill never disagree about where they are being put back.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function guidedResumeNotice(stored: GuidedDrillSession | null): string | undefined {\\r\\n  if (!stored || stored.status !== \'in_progress\') return undefined;\\r\\n  const total = stored.question_queue.length;\\r\\n  if (total === 0) return undefined;\\r\\n  const answered = stored.attempts.length;\\r\\n  if (answered === 0) return undefined;\\r\\n  const at =\\r\\n    guidedResumeIndex(\\r\\n      stored.question_queue,\\r\\n      stored.current_index,\\r\\n      stored.attempts.map((a) => a.qid),\\r\\n    ) + 1;\\r\\n  return `Picking up where you left off — question ${at} of ${total}, ${answered} already answered.`;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

The UI does not block on it, but callers that\\r\\n    // must not fabricate a default over real cloud data await ready() first.\\r\\n    // hydrate() never rejects (it catches internally), so ready() always resolves.\\r\\n    this.readyPromise = this.hydrate();\\r\\n\\r\\n    // Best-effort flush on tab close.\\r\\n    if (typeof window !== \'undefined\') {\\r\\n      window.addEventListener(\'beforeunload\', () => {\\r\\n        void this.flush();\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      window.addEventListener(\'visibilitychange\', () => {\\r\\n        if (document.visibilityState === \'hidden\') void this.flush();\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  ready(): Promise<void> {\\r\\n    return this.readyPromise;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  private key(table: string, id: string): string {\\r\\n    return `${table}::${id}`;\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  async hydrate(): Promise<void> {\\r\\n    if (this.hydrated || !this.userId || this.userId === \'anon\') return;\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      const { data, error } = await supabase\\r\\n        .from(\'bootcamp_kv\')\\r\\n        .select(\'table_name, record_id, data\')\\r\\n        .eq(\'user_id\', this.userId);\\r\\n      if (error) {\\r\\n        console.warn(\'[SupabaseSyncedPersistence] hydrate failed\', error.message);\\r\\n        return;\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      for (const row of data ?? []) {\\r\\n        try {\\r\\n          const key = this.key(row.table_name, row.record_id);\\r\\n          // A write made while the cloud request was in flight owns this row.\\r\\n          // && updatedAt(local) > updatedAt(row.data)) {\\r\\n            this.pending.set(key, { table: row.table_name, id: row.record_id, data: local });\\r\\n            this.scheduleFlush();\\r\\n }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  private scheduleFlush(): void {\\r\\n    if (this.flushTimer) clearTimeout(this.flushTimer);\\r\\n    this.flushTimer = setTimeout(() => {\\r\\n      void this.flush();\\r\\n };\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport type { GuidedWorkEvidence, HomeworkWorkEvidence } from \'./studentWorkEvidence\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * An assignment is an issued instance of work, not the content it points to.\\r\\n * The same drill or activity can be assigned more than once, and each issue\\r\\n * must keep its own status and place in the student\'s history.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport type StudentWorkSource =\\r\\n  | \'homework_assignments\'\\r\\n  | \'ta_assignments\'\\r\\n  | \'guided_drill_assignments\'\\r\\n  | \'activity_assignments\'\\r\\n  | \'classroom_items\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type {\\r\\n  return `${sourceTable}:${assignmentId}`;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Viewing instructions or an assignment does not start work. */\\r\\nexport function guidedWorkStatus(row: {\\r\\n  status: return \'assigned\';\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Homework is opened on first render; only a saved answer begins it. */\\r\\nexport function homeworkWorkStatus(row: {\\r\\n  status: \'assigned\' | \'in_progress\' | \'completed\';\\r\\n  completed_at: string | null;\\r\\n}, saved?: HomeworkWorkEvidence): StudentWorkStatus {\\r\\n  if (row.status === \'completed\' && row.completed_at) return \'completed\';\\r\\n  if (saved?.savedAnswerCount && saved.status === \'completed\') return \'completed\';\\r\\n  if (saved?.savedAnswerCount) return \'in_progress\';\\r\\n  return opens. */\\r\\nexport function activityWorkStatus(row: {\\r\\n  status: \'assigned\' | \'in_progress\' | \'completed\' | \'abandoned\';\\r\\n  completed_at: string | null;\\r\\n}, hasSavedResponse: boolean): StudentWorkStatus {\\r\\n  if (row.status === \'completed\' && row.completed_at) return \'completed\';\\r\\n  return hasSavedResponse ? \'in_progress\' : \'assigned\';\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

The caller decides\\r\\n * what to do about that; this never invents a zero, because a zero here reads\\r\\n * as \\"answered everything wrong\\" on the score report.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function readLrLegResult(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  pt: number,\\r\\n  section: number,\\r\\n  startedAtIso: string,\\r\\n  identity?: PtLegIdentity,\\r\\n): Promise<LegResult | null> {\\r\\n  let query = supabase\\r\\n    .from(\'section_history\')\\r\\n    .select(\'initial_score, initial_total, total_questions, total_time_ms, time_taken_ms, completed_at\')\\r\\n    .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\\r\\n    .eq(\'pt\', pt)\\r\\n    .eq(\'section\', section);\\r\\n  query = identity\\r\\n    ? query.eq(\'practice_test_run_id\', identity.runId).eq(\'practice_test_leg\', identity.legIndex)\\r\\n    : query.gte(\'completed_at\', startedAtIso);\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await query\\r\\n    .order(\'completed_at\', { ascending: false })\\r\\n    .limit(1);\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (error || !data || data.length === 0) return null;\\r\\n  const row = data[0];\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    correct: row.initial_score ?? 0,\\r\\n    answered: row.initial_total ?? row.total_questions ?? 0,\\r\\n    timeMs: row.total_time_ms ?? row.time_taken_ms ?? 0,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Read one finished RC leg out of `rc_drill_sessions` + `rc_attempts`.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Matched on the passage set rather than a section number: RC sessions record\\r\\n * which passages were sat, not which section they came from.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport async function readRcLegResult(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  passageIds: number[],\\r\\n  startedAtIso: string,\\r\\n  identity?: PtLegIdentity,\\r\\n): Promise<LegResult | null> {\\r\\n  let query = supabase\\r\\n    .from(\'rc_drill_sessions\')\\r\\n    .select(\'id, passage_ids, completed_at, config\')\\r\\n    .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\\r\\n    .eq(\'status\', \'completed\');\\r\\n  query = identity\\r\\n    ? query.eq(\'practice_test_run_id\', identity.runId).eq(\'practice_test_leg\', identity.legIndex)\\r\\n    : query.gte(\'completed_at\', startedAtIso);\\r\\n  const { data: sessions, error } = await query\\r\\n    .order(\'completed_at\', { ascending: false })\\r\\n    .limit(5);\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (error || !sessions || sessions.length === 0) return null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const wanted = [...passageIds].sort((a, b) => a - b).join(\',\');\\r\\n  const session = sessions.find(\\r\\n    (s) => [...(s.passage_ids ?? [])].sort((a, b) => a - b).join(\',\') === wanted,\\r\\n  );\\r\\n  if (!session) return null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { data: attempts, error: attemptsError } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\'rc_attempts\')\\r\\n    .select(\'selected, is_correct, time_ms\')\\r\\n    .eq(\'session_id\', session.id)\\r\\n    .eq(\'user_id\', userId)\\r\\n    .eq(\'is_first_attempt\', true);\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (attemptsError || !attempts) return null;\\r\\n  const config = session.config as { clockElapsedSeconds?: unknown } | null;\\r\\n  return projectRcLegResult(attempts, config?.clockElapsedSeconds);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function readLegResult(\\r\\n  userId: string,\\r\\n  run: PracticeTestRun,\\r\\n  leg: PracticeTestLeg,\\r\\n): Promise<LegResult | null> {\\r\\n  if (!leg.startedAt) return null;\\r\\n  // Account-backed runs require an exact leg link. Timestamp proximity could\\r\\n  // score an unrelated section taken in another tab or on another device.\\r\\n  const identity = run.revision === undefined\\r\\n    ? undefined\\r\\n    : { runId: run.id, legIndex: run.currentLeg };\\r\\n  return leg.kind === \'lr\'\\r\\n    ? readLrLegResult(userId, run.pt, leg.section, leg.startedAt, identity)\\r\\n    : readRcLegResult(userId, leg.passageIds ?? [], leg.startedAt, identity);\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"423ce2","wall_time_seconds":0.0000381,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3929,"output":"# Timed work must commit through one account transaction\\r\\n\\r\\nDate: 2026-09-30\\r\\n\\r\\nStatus: **Proposed implementation contract. Not implemented or deployed.** This is the backend follow-up for competitor-audit ticket #167. It does not close #167 or claim full second-device, outage, or revoked-access acceptance. The frontend recovery fixes can ship independently.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Why the existing saves leave a race\\r\\n\\r\\nThe parent PrepTest save already compares revision, active device, and in-progress status in one conditional UPDATE. Its LR and RC children check the parent separately before writing their own rows. A second browser can take over after that check and before the child write. Browser-local write queues do not serialize two devices. Ownership RLS prevents writing another student\'s work; it does not prevent an old browser from writing the same student\'s work.\\r\\n\\r\\nEvidence below is from repository source. **SQL files describe intended schema, not verified live DDL.** An operational table read or successful ordinary save also does not prove all constraints, policies, or race protection are installed. Record a live schema/policy inventory and independent CAS test before rollout.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Source | Existing behavior and limitation |\\r\\n| --- | --- |\\r\\n| `src/lib/practiceTestStore.ts:271`, `:302`, `:244` | `saveAccountPracticeTestRun` conditionally writes parent revision/device/status; `continueAccountPracticeTestHere` claims the device with parent CAS. `isActivePracticeTestDevice` is a separate read used by child writers. |\\r\\n| `src/lib/drillResumeSession.ts:56`, `:76` | `assertActiveTestLeg` checks the parent device; `syncSectionToAccount` separately upserts the LR frozen snapshot into `bootcamp_kv`. Official section writes have local serialization and recovery copies. |\\r\\n| `src/features/rc/api/rcProgress.ts:93`, `:154`, `:228`, `:184` | `assertActivePracticeTestSession`, `writeSessionClock`, `recordAttempt`, and `completeSession` use separate parent reads and child writes. RC clock serialization is per browser. |\\r\\n| `src/pages/PracticeTestRun.tsx:380`, `:388` | The parent stores break start time; failed-save recovery freezes remaining time locally. The break heartbeat currently runs at 15 seconds. |\\r\\n| `src/components/AssignmentGate.tsx:32`, `:299` | Route admission accepts a feature flag or item authorization, with cached assignment reads. It is not a server write contract and cannot provide immediate revocation enforcement. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260829120000_practice_test_runs.sql:135`, `:237`, `:245`, `:249`, `:281` | Parent owner UPDATE, RC owner FK, one LR result/RC session per leg, and RC first-attempt uniqueness. Owner UPDATE does not enforce terminal transitions, device ownership, or current access. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260927010000_practice_test_account_sync.sql:3` | Adds `section_seconds` and `active_device_id`. Does not add a clock lease or transactional child writes. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260923010300_practice_test_assignments.sql:41`, `:63` | Defines `can_sit_practice_test` and applies it to parent INSERT. Existing owner UPDATE remains broader. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260603142340_2aa5f9d2-63ec-4736-af91-7a426457f9de.sql:31` | KV owner UPDATE permits account-owned JSON snapshots without active-leg or lease checks. |\\r\\n| `supabase/migrations/20260806000000_rc_drilling.sql:35` | RC owner policies scope work to the owner. They do not implement clock/device/access state transitions. |\\r\\n\\r\\nReuse these tables and constraints. The existing `abandon_stale_practice_tests` RPC is cleanup, not an atomic checkpoint API. No relevant timed checkpoint RPC was found in these files.\\r\\n\\r\\n## One versioned command owns parent and child writes\\r\\n\\r\\nImplement `commit_timed_work_v1` as a database transaction. For a PrepTest, lock its existing `practice_test_runs` parent row. For standalone timed LR/RC, lock a small normalized `timed_work_controls` row keyed to the immutable owned LR snapshot or RC session identity. This companion row prevents generic KV JSON from becoming the source of device ownership or source authorization.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe command accepts a work identity, expected revision, writer epoch/token, command UUID, operation, and a typed operation payload. It does not accept an arbitrary student owner. Derive the actor from `auth.uid()`.\\r\\n\\r\\nAllowed operations: claim, start, checkpoint, pause, resume, complete, abandon; PrepTests additionally start/finish break and complete/advance leg. Validate each operation separately rather than accepting an unrestricted parent/JSON patch.\\r\\n\\r\\nWithin the transaction:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Lock the control/parent row for `auth.uid()` and validate contract version.\\r\\n2. Check current access against live source rows, expected revision, active writer epoch, allowed state transition, and exact current leg.\\r\\n3. Check child ownership and immutable source attribution. A PrepTest child must belong to this parent, owner, leg, section kind, and frozen section definition.\\r\\n4. Update the official LR frozen snapshot or the RC attempt, elapsed clock, and current position together with the control state. Reject missing/unavailable question identities rather than drawing a new queue.\\r\\n5. On completion, acknowledge the official result and advance the parent atomically. No next leg or final score becomes available before this transaction commits.\\r\\n6. Increment revision, store the command receipt, and return the authoritative control/parent plus child checkpoint and server time.\\r\\n\\r\\nMaintain durable command receipts keyed by owner, work identity, and command UUID, with a payload hash and committed result. Same UUID/same payload returns the acknowledged result after a lost response. Same UUID/different payload is rejected. Check a matching receipt before rejecting a replay for stale revision: a legitimately committed command remains acknowledged after later operations. A new stale command never overwrites newer work.\\r\\n\\r\\nLR analytics and WAJ remain projections of acknowledged work. If they cannot be written inside closeout, enqueue idempotent follow-up work transactionally and keep retries independent of whether the official leg can advance. RC attempts retain existing UUIDs and first-attempt uniqueness. Decide explicitly which projections must finish before results appear; do not silently weaken the current closeout contract.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Every save rechecks access without changing the actor\\r\\n\\r\\nPrepTest commands reuse `can_sit_practice_test(auth.uid(), parent.pt)`, including current unlock/archive state and the Practice tests feature flag. Validate the active exact leg independently of the feature flag. A broad feature grant does not authorize an arbitrary child under a different parent or leg.\\r\\n\\r\\nStandalone LR/RC control creation freezes its source discriminator and source identity. Each protected write requires the current drill feature grant or a current, owned assignment that authorizes exactly that section/session source. Validate unlock/archive/deletion state and source membership server-side. Opening unrelated assigned work must not grant access to arbitrary sections. Do not infer authorization from a caller-supplied queue, generic KV namespace, or cached route result.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe standalone source mapping must be inventoried and implemented for every supported launch before enforcement is enabled. Unsupported source kinds fail explicitly. Teacher/admin read access continues through existing SELECT policies. No teacher mutation impersonates the student\'s writer; any future teacher recovery operation needs its own audited authorization contract.\\r\\n\\r\\nAccess revocation denies new checkpoint, resume, claim, and completion commands. Preserve prior answers and recovery copies. Historical owned result reads remain available according to the existing result-access policy. Retrying an already committed command can acknowledge its existing receipt but cannot create further work after revocation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The server needs a clock and an expiring writer lease\\r\\n\\r\\nStore phase, accumulated elapsed milliseconds, server running anchor, lease expiry, writer epoch, and contract version. For PrepTests the clock state identifies the exact leg or break; section duration remains the frozen `section_seconds` chosen at creation. Standalone controls hold their own frozen timing. Use server time for accepted state transitions; never replace saved time with a smaller client value.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Proposed, not locked:** heartbeat every 15 seconds, writer lease 45 seconds. This follows the current break heartbeat cadence and tolerates two missed heartbeats before expiry. It bounds stale-writer acceptance and server-visible disconnect behavior without treating a single transient request failure as a permanent takeover. Verify the tolerance against real latency and teaching requirements before adopting it.\\r\\n\\r\\nA successful pause checkpoints elapsed time and removes the running anchor. Resume creates a new anchor only after acknowledgment. When the lease expires, the authoritative clock stops at its expiry boundary and the work requires explicit recovery/claim. A claim increments the writer epoch so all old in-flight writes are rejected under the same parent lock.\\r\\n\\r\\n**An offline pause that never reaches the server cannot be globally instantaneous.** Another device can only observe the last acknowledged checkpoint and lease expiry. Document that interval in acceptance evidence. A local paused clock remains a recovery copy, not proof that the server paused at the same instant. Reconcile unacknowledged elapsed time deliberately and monotonically; do not auto-replay local answers over a newer writer\'s work.\\r\\n\\r\\nAn explicit takeover may invalidate an unexpired lease if the existing UX permits it. The command must still CAS the latest revision and increment the epoch atomically. Expiry must never reset timing or grant a fresh question queue.\\r\\n\\r\\n## RPC adoption must also close direct-write bypasses\\r\\n\\r\\nFor protected contract-version rows, deny authenticated direct parent UPDATE and direct writes to attributed LR KV records, RC session/attempt rows, and official section results. Cover INSERT as well as UPDATE so a caller cannot recreate a child under a new identity. Apply the rule to the OLD row as well as NEW attribution so clearing a protected foreign key cannot bypass enforcement.\\r\\n\\r\\nAn RPC alone is insufficient while broad owner policies still allow the same writes. Permissive policies are ORed; inspect and replace conflicting policies rather than adding a stricter policy beside them. Keep ordinary non-timed/non-protected bootcamp progress behavior intact.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse tightly scoped security-definer functions only where required to perform these protected writes. Restrict execution to authenticated callers, fix the search path, qualify objects, and explicitly verify `auth.uid()`, ownership, source access, writer epoch, and operation payload. No service-role credential goes to the browser. A transaction-local enforcement mechanism, if used, must not be forgeable by authenticated callers.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Roll out without erasing old sittings\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Inventory live columns, functions, constraints, policies, and grants. Verify the existing parent CAS with two ordinary authenticated clients. Record differences from repository SQL before applying changes.\\r\\n2. Add nullable/versioned clock state, standalone control rows, command receipts, and RPCs. Leave historical completed records readable and do not rewrite their timing or answers.\\r\\n3. Add frontend adapters that consume authoritative RPC responses. New timed work uses contract v1. Existing active work enters through an explicit adoption/claim transaction that preserves owner, frozen queue, acknowledged answers, current position, accommodation timing, and completion state.\\r\\n4. Enable protected direct-write restrictions for v1 work. Old browser clients encountering adopted work must fail safely and require refresh; they cannot keep using the legacy writer.\\r\\n5. Complete race, outage, revocation, and compatibility acceptance before widening adoption. No terminal-state reopening, deletion, synthetic timestamps, or silent conversion of an unknown-owner device copy.\\r\\n\\r\\nLegacy active work still using the old protocol does not carry the new cross-device guarantee. Keep #167 open until its supported active paths have adopted the contract or are explicitly excluded with truthful UI behavior. Deploy forward; never roll main back to bypass enforcement.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Acceptance proves the transaction rather than the happy path\\r\\n\\r\\n| Scenario | Required result |\\r\\n| --- | --- |\\r\\n| Two clients submit the same expected revision | Exactly one new command commits; the other gets conflict with authoritative state. |\\r\\n| Takeover races an LR/RC save | Parent lock and epoch decide order; no old-writer child commit after successful takeover. |\\r\\n| Direct protected INSERT/UPDATE or attribution clearing | Denied for ordinary authenticated owner and other account. |\\r\\n| Old epoch, wrong owner/source/leg, terminal parent | No child or parent mutation; saved work remains intact. |\\r\\n| Access is revoked after route admission | New checkpoint/claim/resume/completion denied despite cached route authorization. |\\r\\n| Response lost after commit | Same command UUID/payload returns one acknowledged answer/position/result without duplicate advancement. |\\r\\n| Replayed UUID with changed payload | Rejected; acknowledged receipt and result unchanged. |\\r\\n| A later valid command precedes retry of an old receipt | Old committed operation is acknowledged without restoring its older state. |\\r\\n| RC first-attempt insert races | One first attempt per session/question, UUID retries preserve the original answer. |\\r\\n| Pause/break save fails, reload, then second-device claim | Account clock/lease state is consistent; local backup cannot silently reset remaining time. |\\r\\n| Unsent offline pause | Server stops at documented expiry boundary; no claim of immediate global pause. |\\r\\n| Lost heartbeat and stale browser return | Questions remain gated until claim/recovery acknowledgment; elapsed time does not decrease. |\\r\\n| Leg closeout or final-score write fails midway | No partial advance/score; retry finishes the same leg exactly once. |\\r\\n| Older frontend resumes adopted work | Protected raw writes fail safely; refresh preserves acknowledged records. |\\r\\n| Teacher views records | Same owner/source work is visible through allowed reads; teacher identity never becomes the student writer. |\\r\\n\\r\\nTest standalone LR and standalone RC separately from PrepTest LR/RC and the break. Use existing owned tester fixtures, ordinary authenticated accounts, and controlled request failures. Do not substitute service-role success for permission evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Deployment needs immediate route and credit approval\\r\\n\\r\\nThis batch contains the implementation plan only. It does not contain schema changes, RPC code, frontend RPC adapters, or a backend deployment.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore starting a Lovable Cloud backend deployment, confirm the deployment route with Joshua. Deploy managed Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has been migrated to a separately managed project. Any action that spends or may spend Lovable credits needs Joshua\'s explicit confirmation immediately before that action, even though frontend releases were broadly authorized. Free publication controls do not authorize a credit-using backend action.\\r\\n\\r\\nPrepare the reviewed migration/RPC code and test evidence first so that approval concerns a concrete deployment. Generate database types from the deployed backend afterward; do not hand-edit generated types. Report separately: code prepared, backend deployed, frontend switched, live acceptance verified.\\r\\n"}}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b3e961","wall_time_seconds":1.3812,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":918,"output":"}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction pt118CorrectAnswer(qid: string): string | undefined {\\r\\n  const match = /^PT118-S([12])-Q(\\\\d+)$/.exec(qid);\\r\\n  if (!match) return undefined;\\r\\n  const section = Number(match[1]);\\r\\n  const number = Number(match[2]);\\r\\n  if (section === 1) return PT118_S1_ANSWERS[number];\\r\\n  if (section === 2) return ({ 1: \'D\', 5: \'A\' } as Record<number, string>)[number];\\r\\n  return undefined;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Remap pre-repair PT118 resume IDs once, preserving the student\'s place by content. */\\r\\nexport function migratePracticeSession(row: PracticeSession): PracticeSession {\\r\\n  if ((row.data_version ?? 0) >= PRACTICE_SESSION_DATA_VERSION) return row;\\r\\n  // These namespaces are written by the Drill runner, whose displayed LR\\r\\n  // content comes from the local JSON QuestionBank. An unknown producer may\\r\\n  // have used the already-canonical catalog, so its IDs are not safe to shift.\\r\\n  if (!row.pool_slug.startsWith(\'drill::\') && !row.pool_slug.startsWith(\'guided::\')) return row;\\r\\n  const oldCurrent = row.question_queue[row.current_index];\\r\\n  const mappedCurrent = oldCurrent ? migratePt118Qid(oldCurrent) : null;\\r\\n  const seen = new Set<string>();\\r\\n  const questionQueue = row.question_queue.flatMap((qid) => {\\r\\n    const mapped = migratePt118Qid(qid);\\r\\n    if (!mapped || seen.has(mapped)) return [];\\r\\n    seen.add(mapped);\\r\\n    return [mapped];\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  const seenAttempts = new Set<string>();\\r\\n  const attempts = row.attempts.flatMap((attempt) => {\\r\\n    const qid = migratePt118Qid(attempt.qid);\\r\\n    if (!qid || seenAttempts.has(qid)) return [];\\r\\n    seenAttempts.add(qid);\\r\\n    const answer = pt118CorrectAnswer(qid);\\r\\n    return [{ ...attempt, qid, correct: answer ? attempt.selected_answer === answer : attempt.correct }];\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  const currentIndex = mappedCurrent && questionQueue.includes(mappedCurrent)\\r\\n    ? questionQueue.indexOf(mappedCurrent)\\r\\n    : Math.min(row.current_index, Math.max(questionQueue.length - 1, 0));\\r\\n  const regulationAnsweredQids = row.regulation_answered_qids && [\\r\\n    ...new Set(row.regulation_answered_qids.map(migratePt118Qid).filter((qid): qid is string => !!qid)),\\r\\n  ];\\r\\n  return PracticeSession.parse({\\r\\n    ...row,\\r\\n    data_version: PRACTICE_SESSION_DATA_VERSION,\\r\\n    question_queue: questionQueue,\\r\\n    current_index: currentIndex,\\r\\n    attempts,\\r\\n    regulation_answered_qids: regulationAnsweredQids,\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Deterministic per-user, per-pool record id, so every read and write lands on\\r\\n * the same row without a lookup. Mirrors progressIdFor\'s FNV-1a approach but\\r\\n * hashes user + slug together.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function practiceSessionIdFor(userId: string, poolSlug: string): string {\\r\\n  const input = `${userId}::${poolSlug}`;\\r\\n  let h = 0x811c9dc5;\\r\\n  for (let i = 0; i < input.length; i++) {\\r\\n    h ^= input.charCodeAt(i);\\r\\n    h = Math.imul(h, 0x01000193);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  let h2 = 0x811c9dc5;\\r\\n  for (let i = input.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {\\r\\n    h2 ^= input.charCodeAt(i);\\r\\n    h2 = Math.imul(h2, 0x01000193);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const hex = (h >>> 0).toString(16).padStart(8, \'0\');\\r\\n  const hex2 = (h2 >>> 0).toString(16).padStart(8, \'0\');\\r\\n  return `00000000-0000-4000-8000-${hex}${hex2.slice(0, 4)}`;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One writer per row at a time.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Both writers below read the row and then write it, with an await in between.\\r\\n * The drill runner fires a snapshot on every answer and a completion at the\\r\\n * end, and nothing stopped a snapshot that had already read the row from\\r\\n * writing it AFTER the completion had — which put a finished section back to\\r\\n * in_progress. Chaining each row\'s writes behind the previous one keeps them\\r\\n * in the order they were asked for. Writes to different rows still overlap.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"4d25ee","wall_time_seconds":0.8440527,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1248,"output":"diff --git
